APPENDIX IV 
 
Large Load Interconnection Process and Timeline
--- PAGE 56 ---
2 
 
Appendix IV 
to the DCRT TO Tariff 
 
Large Load Interconnection Process and Timeline 
1.1 
Applicability. This Appendix IV shall govern all requests for Interconnection 
submitted by Large Load Interconnection Customers seeking to interconnect a Large 
Load directly to the Participating TO’s Transmission System. Participating TO shall 
coordinate with the CAISO to implement the interconnection of a Large Load as needed 
pursuant to Section 4.3 to this Appendix IV. Coordination with the CAISO pursuant to 
Section 4.3 of this Appendix IV may require that Participating TO, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer and the CAISO negotiate revised deadlines for the 
processing of the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s interconnection request. 
Such revised deadlines will be included in the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Large Load System Impact Study Agreement and Large Load Facilities Study 
Agreement included with these procedures as Attachments 1 and 2 to this Appendix IV, 
respectively.  
1.2 
Application Required. A Large Load Interconnection Customer shall submit a written 
Large Load Interconnection Application that provides the information required in 
Section 1.4 to this Appendix IV. The Participating TO shall time-stamp the application 
to establish study priority and send a copy to any other transmission providers whose 
transmission facilities potentially may be impacted by the interconnection of the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer’s Large Load based on Participating TO’s review of 
the application. Participating TO will also provide the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s application to the CAISO pursuant to Section 4.3.  
1.3 
Process Overview. Participating TO’s processing of a Large Load Interconnection 
Application will proceed in four stages: (1) Sufficiency Review of the Large Load 
Interconnection Application; (2) Large Load System Impact Study; (3), Large Load 
Facilities Study; and (4) negotiation of a Large Load Interconnection Agreement based 
on the pro forma agreement provided as Attachment 3 to this Appendix IV. In the first 
stage, Participating TO will review the Large Load Interconnection Application for 
completeness and sufficiency of the provided information, verify the submitted 
information, and provide an opportunity to cure any deficiencies found. In the second 
phase, Participating TO will execute the Large Load System Impact Study Agreement 
included as Attachment 1 to this Appendix IV with the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer; perform the Large Load System Impact Study; submit the study results to 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer; and conduct a study review meeting with 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer. In the third phase, Participating TO will 
execute the Large Load Facilities Study Agreement included as Attachment 2 to this 
Appendix IV and Participating TO will perform the Large Load Facilities Study to 
address detailed engineering design; submit the study results to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer; and conduct a final study review meeting with the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer. In the fourth phase, Participating TO will negotiate 
and execute a Large Load Interconnection Agreement with the Large Load
--- PAGE 57 ---
3 
 
Interconnection Customer. Participating TO will coordinate with the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to facilitate any studies required to determine the impact of 
the Large Load Interconnection Application on Affected Systems.  
1.4 
Interconnection Application Contents. A Large Load Interconnection Application 
from a Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide the following: 
(i) 
The identity, address, telephone number, and email address for the contacts 
for the entity requesting Interconnection for the relevant Large Load; 
(ii) 
A signed copy of a Large Load System Impact Study Agreement, ready for 
execution by Participating TO; 
(iii) 
At least three (3) dates/times the Large Load Interconnection Customer is 
available to meet with Participating TO within one (1) week of the Large 
Load Interconnection Application’s submission;  
(iv) 
The Point(s) of Interconnection to the Participating TO’s system 
contemplated by the applicant; 
(v) 
The resultant (or new) maximum amount of Interconnection capacity 
contemplated by the Large Load Interconnection Customer; 
(vi) 
The proposed date for energizing the Large Load and the term of Large Load 
Interconnection Service;  
(vii) The electrical location of the source of the power (if known) to be transmitted 
pursuant to the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s request for 
Interconnection. If the source of the power is not known, a system purchase 
will be assumed; 
(viii) The electrical location of the ultimate load (if known). If the location of the 
load is not known, a system sale will be assumed; 
(ix) 
Energization plans and expected in-service dates; 
(x) 
The following physical characteristics of the prospective Large Load, 
sufficient to characterize expected load behavior, including: 
a. 
Load type 
b. 
Variable or flat load designation 
c. 
Expected hourly operating profile 
d. 
Location of service 
e. 
Point of delivery
--- PAGE 58 ---
4 
 
f. 
Connected capacity 
g. 
Any market participation status or expected participation in demand 
response programs 
h. 
Maximum operating demand 
i. 
Minimum operating demand 
j. 
Curtailment capability 
k. 
Expected hourly flexibility capability 
l. 
Ramp-up capability 
m. 
Ramp-down capability 
n. 
Preliminary site layout 
o. 
Modelling inputs for steady-state and transient studies 
p. 
Location, size, and connection of every transformer and large motor 
q. 
Evidence of site control 
r. 
Attestation by officer of accuracy of submitted Large Load 
Interconnection Application 
(xi) 
Co-located resource information, if applicable: 
a. 
Presence of co-located generation 
b. 
Whether the resource will be fully Behind-the-Meter or may export to 
the CAISO Controlled Grid 
c. 
Resource type 
d. 
Nameplate capacity 
e. 
Minimum and maximum operating levels 
f. 
Market participation status 
g. 
Associated status resource identifiers 
h. 
All information required by the Participating TO and, as applicable, the 
CAISO in the Large Generator Interconnection Application
--- PAGE 59 ---
5 
 
(xii) 
Such other information as the Participating TO reasonably requires to 
process the Large Load Interconnection Application. 
1.4.2 Large Load Interconnection Application Fee. A Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall submit a non-refundable application fee of two-hundred-
thousand dollars ($200,000).  
1.4.3 Amendment to Completed Large Load Interconnection Application. A 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be limited to only amending its 
Large Load Interconnection Application once.  Additional amendments will be 
at Participating TO’s discretion, to be permitted on a not unduly discriminatory 
basis and without undue preference or advantage, and Participating TO’s 
agreement for Large Load Interconnection Customer to make additional 
amendment shall not be unreasonably withheld.  Such amendment shall occur 
no later than ten (10) Business Days after the date the Participating TO tenders 
the Large Load System Impact Study report. Specifically, a Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may submit an amendment to its Interconnection 
Application to reflect a revised configuration for its Large Load. In the event a 
Large Load Interconnection Customer amends its Large Load Interconnection 
Application, it will be responsible for any additional study costs that result from 
that amendment. 
1.5 
Withdrawal of Large Load Interconnection Application. A Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may withdraw its Large Load Interconnection Application 
at any time by providing notice to Participating TO in writing. A withdrawal shall not 
result in the refund of any previously paid application fees.  
2. Application Processing and Timeline. The Large Load Interconnection Application will be 
processed under the procedures and timeline described below: 
2.1 
Sufficiency Review. Within five (5) Business Days of receipt of a Large Load 
Interconnection Application and application fee, Participating TO will complete its 
sufficiency review of the Large Load Interconnection Application and provide the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer with either a notice of sufficiency or a deficiency 
letter for the Large Load Interconnection Application, via email. A deficiency letter will 
specify the necessary changes to meet the Large Load Interconnection Application 
requirements. 
2.1.1 Cure of Deficiency. If a Large Load Interconnection Customer receives a 
deficiency letter, it will have three (3) Business Days to re-submit a revised 
Large Load Interconnection Application curing the deficiencies identified in the 
deficiency letter.  
2.1.2 Review of Resubmitted Large Load Interconnection Application. If the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer receives a deficiency letter and timely 
re-submits a revised Large Load Interconnection Application, within five (5) 
calendar days, Participating TO will conduct another sufficiency review of the
--- PAGE 60 ---
6 
 
resubmitted application and notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer of 
the sufficiency or deficiency of the resubmitted application. A second finding 
of deficiency will result in the application being deemed withdrawn.   
2.2 
Large Load System Impact Study. Within seven (7) calendar days of approving a 
completed Large Load Interconnection Application, Participating TO and Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall execute the Large Load System Impact Study 
Agreement included in the initial Large Load Interconnection Application and conduct 
an initial Scoping Meeting. The system impact study will consist of a short circuit 
analysis, a stability analysis, a power flow analysis, and any other studies that are 
deemed necessary. The system impact study shall state the assumptions upon which it 
is based, state the results of the analyses, and provide the requirement or potential 
impediments to providing the requested Large Load Interconnection Service, including 
a preliminary indication of the cost and length of time that would be necessary to 
correct any problems identified in those analyses and implement the Interconnection.   
The Large Load System Impact Study shall provide a list of Large Load 
Interconnection Facilities and Network Upgrades that are required to interconnect the 
relevant Large Load and provide a non-binding good faith estimate of cost 
responsibility and the amount of construction time required. The Large Load System 
Impact Study shall also identify any Affected Systems that Participating TO must 
coordinate with in order to interconnect the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  
2.2.1 Large Load System Impact Study Fee. If the Large Load System Impact 
Study costs exceed the Large Load Interconnection Application Fee, the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer shall reimburse the Participating TO for the 
actual study costs that exceed the initial application fee of two-hundred-
thousand dollars ($200,000).  
2.2.2 Large Load System Impact Study Timeline. Within thirty (30) calendar days 
of execution of the Large Load System Impact Study Agreement, Participating 
TO, using reasonable efforts, will perform the Large Load System Impact 
Study, prepare and submit a study report to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, and provide the Large Load Interconnection Customer with an initial 
draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement reflecting the results of the Large 
Load System Impact Study. This draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement 
will be revised based on the Large Load Facilities Study described in Section 
2.3. Within seven calendar (7) days of submitting the Large Load System 
Impact Study report to the Large Load Interconnection Customer, using 
reasonable efforts, Participating TO will schedule a meeting with the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer and the CAISO, as applicable pursuant to 
Section 4.3, to review the results of the Large Load System Impact Study. 
2.3 
Large Load Facilities Study. The Large Load Facilities Study will be used to create 
the detailed design of the interconnection facilities used to interconnect the Large Load 
to the Participating TO’s facilities, based on the findings of the Large Load System 
Impact Study.
--- PAGE 61 ---
7 
 
2.3.1 Large Load Facilities Study Timeline. The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer will have seven (7) calendar days after receiving the Large Load 
System Impact Study results to execute a Large Load Facilities Study 
Agreement with Participating TO or its Large Load Interconnection Application 
will be deemed withdrawn. Within thirty (30) calendar days of execution of the 
Large Load Facilities Study Agreement, using reasonable efforts, Participating 
TO will conduct the Large Load Facilities Study and submit the study report to 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  
2.3.2 Large Load Facilities Study Fee. The Large Load Interconnection Customer 
shall submit a non-refundable study fee of two-hundred thousand dollars 
($200,000) upon execution of the Large Load Facilities Study Agreement. If the 
Large Load Facilities Study costs exceed the study fee, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall promptly reimburse the Participating TO for the 
actual study costs that exceed the default study fee.  
2.4 
Consideration of Alternative Transmission Technologies. The Large Load System 
Impact Study and Large Load Facilities Study will consider both traditional Network 
Upgrades and Alternative Transmission Technologies when evaluating options for 
reliably interconnecting the Large Load. Alternative Transmission Technologies to be 
studied will include, as applicable, static synchronous compensators, static VAR 
compensators, advanced power flow control devices, transmission switching, 
synchronous condensers, voltage source converters, advanced conductors, and tower 
lifting. The Participating TO’s studies, will select the best available transmission 
solution to interconnect Large Load. If a traditional Network Upgrade solution is 
selected instead of Alternative Transmission Technologies, the Participating TO will 
explain why Alternative Transmission Technologies are not feasible (i.e., would not 
resolve reliability violations identified or meet the relevant planning criteria) or would 
not result in lower costs or a faster timeline for accommodating the interconnection of 
the Large Load. 
2.5 
Large Load Interconnection Agreement.  
2.5.1 Large Load Interconnection Agreement Timeline. Within sixty (60) calendar 
days of receipt of the Large Load Facilities Study report, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall execute a Large Load Interconnection 
Agreement with Participating TO or request that an unexecuted Large Load 
Interconnection Agreement be filed with FERC for resolution of any dispute 
over the terms of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, or the Large Load 
Interconnection Application will be deemed withdrawn.  
2.5.2 Large Load Interconnection Agreement Signatories and Terms. The Large 
Load Interconnection Agreement will be negotiated between the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer and Participating TO, with, the CAISO also signing, 
as applicable pursuant to Section 4.3. The Large Load Interconnection 
Agreement will obligate the Large Load Interconnection Customer to pay the 
cost of interconnecting the Large Load to the Participating TO’s system,
--- PAGE 62 ---
8 
 
including procurement, construction, and commissioning of the interconnection 
facilities based on the terms within the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. 
Participating TO will not perform any procurement or construction associated 
with interconnecting the Large Load until the Large Load Interconnection 
Agreement is executed and all funding identified in the Large Load 
Interconnection Agreement is received. The Large Load Interconnection 
Agreement will address reimbursement to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer for any Network Upgrades. 
2.5.3 Construction of Interconnection Facilities. Based on the designs produced by 
the Large Load Facilities Study, Participating TO will procure necessary 
equipment, and construct the interconnection facilities required to interconnect 
the Large Load to the Participating TO’s system.  
2.6 
Interconnection Financial Security Agreements.  A Large Load Interconnection 
Customer intending to proceed with interconnection shall post financial security 
according to the timelines identified in Attachment 4 to this Appendix IV, or its 
application will be deemed withdrawn.  
2.6.1 Initial Postings. Within ninety (90) calendar days of receipt of the Facilities 
Study report, a Large Load Interconnection Customer shall execute separate 
Interconnection Financial Security Agreements and post financial security for 
each of: 1) its assigned Network Upgrades; and 2) its Interconnection Facilities. 
2.6.2 Second Postings.  Second postings and amended Interconnection Financial 
Security Agreements are required no later than thirty (30) calendar days prior 
to the date on which the Facilities Study report provides that such amounts will 
be needed to fund long lead time equipment procurement as part of the 
Construction Activities (as defined in Attachment 4) for the Network Upgrades 
(as defined in Attachment 3) or the Participating TO’s Interconnection 
Facilities, whichever is earlier. 
2.6.3 Third Postings.  No later than thirty (30) calendar days after the date on which 
the Participating TO provides notice that Participating TO is obligated to 
provide security needed to fund any additional obligations for Network 
Upgrades (as defined in Attachment 3) or Participating TO’s Interconnection 
Facilities on behalf of the Large Load Interconnection Customer, whichever is 
earlier, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall modify the two separate 
Interconnection Financial Security instruments. 
3. Flexible Interim Load Interconnection Service.  
3.1 
Availability of Flexible Interim Load Interconnection Service. If a Large Load 
System Impact Study conducted for a Large Load Interconnection Customer shows that 
the Large Load cannot be reliably served at its full requested demand until certain 
Network Upgrades are completed, Participating TO may, at the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s request offer FILI Service. Based on the study results,
--- PAGE 63 ---
9 
 
Participating TO, and, as applicable, the CAISO pursuant to Section 4.3, may propose 
a customized plan for the Large Load Interconnection Customer based on the type of 
constraints required to maintain Transmission System reliability. Such constraints may 
require flexibility in demand by the Large Load or any other conditions that 
Participating TO and, as applicable, the CAISO determine are necessary to maintain 
reliability. The Large Load Interconnection Customer may participate in the design of 
the FILI Service constraints. Once the required Network Upgrades necessary to serve 
the Large Load at its full requested demand are complete, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may elect to receive firm service.  The scope of any FILI 
offered to the Large Load Interconnection Customer will be memorialized in the Large 
Load Interconnection Agreement. 
4. Participating TO Obligation to Provide Interconnection Service. 
4.1 
Interconnection to Transmission System.  Interconnection Services for Large Loads 
must be consistent with Good Utility Practice, in conformance with all Applicable 
Reliability Criteria, all applicable statutes, regulations, the Participating TO’s reliability 
criteria and, as applicable, the CAISO reliability criteria for the CAISO Controlled 
Grid. The Participating TO will not provide Large Load Interconnection Services to the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer if doing so would impair reliability. 
4.2 
Energization.  The Participating TO shall not be obligated to energize, nor shall the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer be entitled to have its interconnection to the 
Participating TO’s system energized, unless and until a Large Load Interconnection 
Agreement has been executed, or filed at FERC pursuant to Section 2.5.1 and becomes 
effective and such Large Load Interconnection Customer has demonstrated to the 
Participating TO’s reasonable satisfaction that it has complied with all of the 
requirements of the of the Participating TO’s Tariff, and, as applicable, the CAISO 
Tariff. 
4.3 
Coordination with the CAISO on Interconnection Requests. Participating TO 
reserves the right to coordinate with the CAISO to the extent that the interconnection 
of a Large Load may impact the reliability of the CAISO controlled transmission 
system. The potential injection of any power from a Large Load to the Participating 
TO’s Transmission System, including but not limited to the injection of power to the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System resulting from the colocation of generation 
with the Large Load facility, shall require that the Participating TO coordinate with the 
CAISO to ensure that the Large Load’s interconnection complies with the CAISO 
Tariff.    
4.4 
Coordination with Affected Systems. Participating TO will coordinate with the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer to facilitate any studies required to determine the 
impact of the Large Load Interconnection Application on Affected Systems. 
Participating TO may provide a copy of the Large Load System Impact Study and Large 
Load Facilities Study results to an Affected System Operator and the Western 
Electricity Coordinating Council. Requests for review and input from Affected System 
Operators or the Western Electricity Coordinating Council may arrive at any time prior
--- PAGE 64 ---
10 
 
to interconnection. If interconnecting the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
requires upgrades to any Affected Systems, Large Load Interconnection Customer must 
enter into a construction and reimbursement agreement with the owner of any Affected 
System and/or other affected owners of portions of the CAISO Controlled Grid that 
govern facilities built on the relevant Affected System, pursuant to the tariff or 
procedures of the Affected System owner.

RECITALS 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer is proposing to develop a Large Load 
Facility or Large Load Facility expansion to an existing Large Load Facility consistent with the 
Large Load Interconnection Application submitted by the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
dated   
; and 
WHEREAS, the Large Load Interconnection Customer desires to interconnect the Large 
Load Facility with the California Independent System Operator Corporation (“CAISO”) 
Controlled Grid pursuant to the Large Load Interconnection Process; and 
WHEREAS, the Large Load Interconnection Customer has requested DCRT to conduct or 
cause to be performed a Large Load System Impact Study to assess the system impact of 
interconnecting the Large Load Facility to the CAISO Controlled Grid and to specify and estimate 
the cost of the equipment, engineering, procurement and construction work needed on the 
Participating TO’s electric system in accordance with Good Utility Practice to physically and 
electrically connect the Large Load Facility to the CAISO Controlled Grid; 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of and subject to the mutual covenants contained 
herein the Parties agree as follows: 
1.0 
When used in this Agreement, with initial capitalization, the terms specified shall 
have the meanings indicated in DCRT’s Federal Energy Regulatory Commission 
(“FERC”)-approved Large Load Interconnection Process in the DCRT Tariff, or 
the Master Definitions Supplement, Appendix A to the CAISO Tariff, as 
applicable.
--- PAGE 67 ---
3 
 
2.0 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer elects and DCRT shall conduct or cause 
to be performed a Large Load System Impact Study in accordance with the DCRT 
Tariff, Appendix IV. 
3.0 
The scope of the applicable Large Load System Impact Study shall be subject to 
the assumptions set forth in Appendices A and B to this Agreement. 
4.0 
The Large Load System Impact Study will be based upon the technical information 
provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer in the Large Load 
Interconnection Application, as may be modified as the result of the Scoping 
Meeting, subject to any modifications in accordance with Section 1.4.2 of the 
DCRT Tariff, Appendix IV and modifications to the proposed Commercial 
Operation Date of the Large Load Facility permitted by the Large Load 
Interconnection Process. DCRT reserves the right to request additional technical 
information from the Large Load Interconnection Customer as may reasonably 
become necessary consistent with Good Utility Practice during the course of the 
Large Load System Impact Study. If the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
modifies its designated Point of Interconnection, Large Load Interconnection 
Application, or the technical information provided therein is modified, the Large 
Load System Impact Study may be modified as specified in the DCRT Tariff, 
Appendix IV. 
5.0 
The Large Load System Impact Study report shall provide the information specified 
in the Large Load Interconnection Process. 
6.0 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide a Large Load 
Interconnection Application fee for the performance of the Large Load System 
Impact Study in accordance with the provisions of Section 1.4.1 of the Large Load 
Interconnection Process. 
7.0 
Pursuant to Section 4.4 of the Large Load Interconnection Process, DCRT will 
coordinate with the Large Load Interconnection Customer to facilitate any studies 
required to determine the impact of the Large Load Interconnection Application on 
Affected Systems. DCRT may provide a copy of the system impact and facilities 
study results to an Affected System Operator and the Western Electricity 
Coordinating Council. Requests for review and input from Affected System 
Operators or the Western Electricity Coordinating Council may arrive at any time 
prior to interconnection. 
8.0 
Substantial portions of technical data and assumptions used to perform the Large 
Load System Impact Study, such as system conditions, existing and planned

provides the system impact and facilities study report to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer. DCRT shall not be responsible for any additional costs, 
including, without limitation, costs of new or additional facilities, system upgrades,
--- PAGE 68 ---
4 
 
or schedule changes, that may be incurred by the Large Load Interconnection

9.0 
DCRT shall maintain records and accounts of all costs incurred in performing the 
Large Load System Impact Study in sufficient detail to allow verification of all 
costs incurred, including associated overheads. The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall have the right, upon reasonable notice, within a reasonable time at 
DCRT’s offices and at its own expense, to audit DCRT’s records as necessary and 
as appropriate in order to verify costs incurred by DCRT. Any audit requested by 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be completed, and written notice of 
any audit dispute provided to DCRT representative, within one hundred eighty 
(180) calendar days following receipt by the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
of DCRT’s notification of the final costs of the Interconnection Study. 
10.0  In accordance with Section 1.5 of the Large Load Interconnection Process, the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer may withdraw its Large Load 
Interconnection Application at any time by written notice to DCRT.  
Within ninety (90) days of receiving the withdrawal notice of the Large Load 
Interconnection Application, DCRT will refund the unused amount of the Large 
Load Interconnection Study Application fee to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer.

DCRT TO Tariff, Appendix IV 
LARGE LOAD INTERCONNECTION AGREEMENT 
[LARGE LOAD INTERCONNECTION CUSTOMER] 
AND 
DCR TRANSMISSION, L.L.C. 
THIS LARGE LOAD INTERCONNECTION AGREEMENT (“LLIA”) is made and 
entered into this ______ day of ________________ 20___, by and among _________________, 
a ________________, organized and existing under the laws of the State/Commonwealth of 
___________ (“Large Load Interconnection Customer” with a Large Load Facility), and DCR 
Transmission, L.L.C., a limited liability company, organized and existing under the laws of the 
State of Delaware (“DCRT” or “Participating TO”). Large Load Interconnection Customer, and 
Participating TO each may be referred to as a “Party” or collectively as the “Parties.”1 
RECITALS 
WHEREAS, the California Independent System Operator Corporation (“CAISO”) 
exercises Operational Control over the CAISO Controlled Grid; and 
WHEREAS, the Participating TO owns, operates, and maintains the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System; and 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer intends to own, lease and/or control 
and operate the Large Load Facility identified as a Large Load Facility in Appendices A and C to 
this LLIA; and 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer, Participating TO, and, as applicable, 
CAISO have agreed to enter into this LLIA for the purpose of interconnecting the Large Load 
Facility with the Participating TO’s Transmission System; and  
WHEREAS, to the extent applicable, Appendix H sets forth the applicable provisions 
relating to impacts (financial, operational, regulatory and otherwise) on the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer arising out of or related to co-located or Behind-the-Meter Generation 
utilizing the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities that may 
necessitate CAISO becoming a signatory to this LLIA;   
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of and subject to the mutual covenants contained 
herein, it is agreed: 
 
1 The California Independent System Operator Corporation will be added as a party to this LLIA as may be required 
pursuant to Appendix H.
--- PAGE 89 ---
2 
When used in this LLIA, terms with initial capitalization that are not defined in Article 1 
shall have the meanings specified in the Article in which they are used.  Further, terms with 
initial capitalization which are used but not defined in the LLIA shall have the meaning given 
them in the Appendices. 
Article 1. Definitions 
1.1 
Capitalized terms used herein that are not defined in this Article 1 are defined in the 
Participating TO Tariff.  
Access Party shall have the meaning set forth in Article 5.11. 
Affected System shall mean an electric system other than the Participating Transmission 
Owners Transmission System that may be affected by the proposed interconnection. 
Affiliate shall mean, with respect to a corporation, partnership or other entity, each such 
other corporation, partnership or other entity that directly or indirectly, through one or more 
intermediaries, controls, is controlled by, or is under common control with, such corporation, 
partnership or other entity. 
Alternative Transmission Technologies shall mean any transmission technology other 
than Network Upgrades that can be used to improve the Participating TO’s Transmission System 
to interconnect an Interconnection Customer or Large Load Interconnection Customer, which 
may include static synchronous compensators, static VAR compensators, advanced power flow 
control devices, transmission switching, synchronous condensers, voltage source converters, 
advanced conductors, and tower lifting.  
Applicable Laws and Regulations shall mean all duly promulgated applicable federal, 
state and local laws, regulations, rules, ordinances, codes, decrees, judgments, directives, or 
judicial or administrative orders, permits and other duly authorized actions of any Governmental 
Authority. 
Applicable Reliability Council shall mean the Western Electricity Coordinating Council 
or its successor. 
Applicable Reliability Standards shall mean the requirements and guidelines of NERC, 
the Applicable Reliability Council, and the Balancing Authority Area of the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System to which the Large Load Facility is directly connected, including 
requirements adopted pursuant to section 215 of the Federal Power Act. 
Balancing Authority shall mean the responsible entity that integrates resource plans 
ahead of time, maintains load-interchange-generation balance within a Balancing Authority 
Area, and supports Interconnection frequency in real time. 
Balancing Authority Area shall mean the collection of generation, transmission, and 
loads within the metered boundaries of the Balancing Authority. The Balancing Authority 
maintains load-resource balance within this area.
--- PAGE 90 ---
3 
Behind-the-Meter Generation shall mean generating facility that delivers energy to load 
without using the transmission system or any distribution facilities. 
Breach shall mean the failure of a Party to perform or observe any material term or 
condition of this LLIA. 
Breaching Party shall mean a Party that is in Breach of this LLIA. 
Business Day shall mean Monday through Friday, excluding federal holidays and the day 
after Thanksgiving Day. 
CAISO Controlled Grid shall mean the system of transmission lines and associated 
facilities of the parties to the Transmission Control Agreement that have been placed under the 
CAISO’s Operational Control. 
CAISO Tariff shall mean the CAISO’s tariff, as filed with FERC, and as amended or 
supplemented from time to time, or any successor tariff. 
Calendar Day shall mean any day including Saturday, Sunday or a federal holiday. 
Co-located Facilities shall mean both the load and generation facilities in a configuration 
where end-use customer load is physically connected to the facilities of an existing or planned 
generating facility on the generator interconnection customer’s side of the point of 
interconnection to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  
Commercial Operation shall mean the status of a (i) Large Load Facility or project 
phase at a Large Load Facility that has commenced demand for electricity, excluding electricity 
used during the construction of the Large Load Facility or (ii) Network Upgrade, when such 
upgrade is fully and finally constructed and can fully operate as intended. 
Commercial Operation Date of a Large Load Facility or project phase shall mean the 
date on which the Large Load Facility or project phase at the Large Load Facility commences 
Commercial Operation as agreed to by the applicable Participating TO, as applicable, the 
CAISO, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer pursuant to Appendices E and H to this 
LLIA, and in accordance with any implementation plan agreed to by the Participating TO and the 
CAISO for multiple individual Large Load Facilities or project phases at a Large Load Facility 
where a Large Load Interconnection Customer intends to establish separate Commercial 
Operation Dates for those Large Load Facilities or project phases. 
Confidential Information shall mean any confidential, proprietary or trade secret 
information of a plan, specification, pattern, procedure, design, device, list, concept, policy or 
compilation relating to the present or planned business of a Party, which is designated as 
confidential by the Party supplying the information, whether conveyed orally, electronically, in 
writing, through inspection, or otherwise, subject to Article 22.1.2. 
Default shall mean the failure of a Breaching Party to cure its Breach in accordance with 
Article 17 of this LLIA.
--- PAGE 91 ---
4 
Distribution System shall mean those non-CAISO-controlled transmission and 
distribution facilities owned by the Participating TO. 
Distribution Upgrades shall mean the additions, modifications, and upgrades to the 
Participating TO’s Distribution System. Distribution Upgrades do not include Interconnection 
Facilities. 
Effective Date shall mean the date on which this LLIA becomes effective upon 
execution by all Parties subject to acceptance by FERC, or if filed unexecuted, upon the date 
specified by FERC. 
Emergency Condition shall mean a condition or situation, that in the judgment of the 
Party making the claim, is imminently likely: (1) to endanger life or property; (2) is imminently 
likely to cause a material adverse effect on the security of, or damage to, the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution System, or the 
electric systems of others to which the Participating TO’s electric system is directly connected; 
or (3) to cause a material adverse effect on the security of, or damage to, the Large Load Facility 
or Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.   
Environmental Law shall mean Applicable Laws and Regulations relating to pollution 
or protection of the environment or natural resources. 
Federal Power Act shall mean the Federal Power Act, as amended, 16 U.S.C. §§ 791a et 
seq. 
FERC shall mean the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission or its successor. 
Flexible Interim Load Interconnection Service (FILI) shall mean the interim 
interconnection service that the Participating TO may offer pursuant to Section 4.1 of the LLIP 
where the Large Load Facility cannot be reliably served at its full requested demand until certain 
Network Upgrades are completed, as further described in Section A-2 of Appendix A to this 
LLIA. 
Force Majeure shall mean any act of God, labor disturbance, act of the public enemy, 
war, insurrection, riot, fire, storm or flood, explosion, breakage or accident to machinery or 
equipment, any order, regulation or restriction imposed by governmental, military or lawfully 
established civilian authorities, or any other cause beyond a Party’s control. A Force Majeure 
event does not include acts of negligence or intentional wrongdoing by the Party claiming Force 
Majeure. 
Good Utility Practice shall mean any of the practices, methods and acts engaged in or 
approved by a significant portion of the electric utility industry during the relevant time period, 
or any of the practices, methods and acts which, in the exercise of reasonable judgment in light 
of the facts known at the time the decision was made, could have been expected to accomplish 
the desired result at a reasonable cost consistent with good business practices, reliability, safety 
and expedition. Good Utility Practice is not intended to be any one of a number of the optimum 
practices, methods, or acts to the exclusion of all others, but rather to be acceptable practices, 
methods, or acts generally accepted in the region.
--- PAGE 92 ---
5 
Governmental Authority shall mean any federal, state, local or other governmental, 
regulatory or administrative agency, court, commission, department, board, or other 
governmental subdivision, legislature, rulemaking board, tribunal, or other governmental 
authority having jurisdiction over the Parties, their respective facilities, or the respective services 
they provide, and exercising or entitled to exercise any administrative, executive, police, or 
taxing authority or power; provided, however, that such term does not include the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, CAISO, Participating TO, or any Affiliate thereof. 
Granting Party shall have the meaning set forth in Article 5.11. 
Hazardous Substances shall mean any chemicals, materials or substances defined as or 
included in the definition of “hazardous substances,” “hazardous wastes,” “hazardous materials,” 
“hazardous constituents,” “restricted hazardous materials,” “extremely hazardous substances,” 
“toxic substances,” “radioactive substances,” “contaminants,” “pollutants,” “toxic pollutants” or 
words of similar meaning and regulatory effect under any applicable Environmental Law, or any 
other chemical, material or substance, exposure to which is prohibited, limited or regulated by 
any applicable Environmental Law. 
In-Service Date shall mean the date upon which the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer reasonably expects it will be ready to begin use of the Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities to draw energy from the Participating Transmission Owner 
Transmission System. 
Interconnection Facilities shall mean the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities 
and the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities. Collectively, 
Interconnection Facilities include all facilities and equipment between the Large Load Facility 
and the Point of Interconnection, including any modification, additions or upgrades that are 
necessary to physically and electrically interconnect the Large Load Facility to the Participating 
TO’s Transmission System. Interconnection Facilities are sole use facilities and shall not include 
Distribution Upgrades, or Network Upgrades. 
Interconnection Financial Security (IFS) shall mean any of the financial instruments 
listed in Attachment 4 to the LLIP that are posted by Large Load Interconnection Customer to 
finance the construction of facilities or Network Upgrades. 
Interconnection Service shall mean the service provided by the Participating TO and, as 
applicable, CAISO associated with interconnecting the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities to the Participating TO’s Transmission System and enabling the 
CAISO Controlled Grid to transmit electric energy and capacity from the  CAISO Controlled 
Grid to the Large Load Facility at the Point of Interconnection, pursuant to the terms of this 
LLIA, the Participating TO’s Transmission Owner Tariff, and, as applicable, the CAISO Tariff. 
IRS shall mean the Internal Revenue Service. 
LLIP means the process for studying load interconnection as set forth in Appendix IV of 
the Participating TO Tariff.
--- PAGE 93 ---
6 
Large Load Facility means a facility whose Point(s) of Interconnection connect to the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System and has a peak load of 50 megawatts (MW) or greater. 
Large Load Facility Point of Change of Ownership shall mean the point, as set forth in 
Appendix A to this LLIA, where the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection 
Facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities. 
Large Load Interconnection Agreement (LLIA) shall have the meaning set forth in the 
Preamble. 
Large Load Interconnection Application shall mean an application for interconnection, 
in the form of Section 1.4 to the LLIP. 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall have the meaning set forth in the 
Preamble. 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities shall mean all 
facilities and equipment, as identified in Appendix A of this LLIA, that are located between the 
Large Load Facility and the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, including any 
modification, addition, or upgrades to such facilities and equipment necessary to physically and 
electrically interconnect the Large Load Facility to the Participating TO’s Transmission System. 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities are sole use facilities. 
Large Load Interconnection Studies shall mean studies performed pursuant to 
Appendix IV of the Participating TO Tariff, including the Large Load System Impact Study and 
the Large Load Facilities Study. 
Loss shall mean any and all damages, losses, and claims, including claims and actions 
relating to injury to or death of any person or damage to property, demand, suits, recoveries, 
costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and all other obligations by or to third parties. 
Metering Equipment shall mean all metering equipment installed or to be installed for 
measuring the power flows to the Large Load Facility pursuant to this LLIA at the metering 
points, including but not limited to instrument transformers, Megawatt-hour-meters, data 
acquisition equipment, transducers, remote terminal unit, communications equipment, phone 
lines, and fiber optics.   
NERC shall mean the North American Electric Reliability Corporation or its successor 
organization. 
Network Upgrades shall be the mean the additions, modifications, and upgrades to the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System at or beyond the Point of Interconnection, identified in 
the Large Load Interconnection Studies, as identified in Appendix A and, as applicable 
Appendix H, to relieve constraints on the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  Alternative 
Transmission Technologies shall be included in the definition of Network Upgrades. 
Operational Control shall mean the rights of the CAISO under the Transmission 
Control Agreement and the CAISO Tariff to direct the parties to the Transmission Control
--- PAGE 94 ---
7 
Agreement how to operate their transmission lines and facilities and other electric plant affecting 
the reliability of those lines and facilities for the purpose of affording comparable non-
discriminatory transmission access and meeting applicable reliability criteria. 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities shall mean all facilities and equipment 
owned, controlled or operated by the Participating TO from the Large Load Facility Point of 
Change of Ownership to the Point of Interconnection as identified in Appendix A to this LLIA, 
including any modifications, additions or upgrades to such facilities and equipment. Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities are sole use facilities and shall not include Distribution 
Upgrades, or Network Upgrades. 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook shall mean the Participating TO’s 
business practice for prospective Large Load Interconnection Customers seeking interconnection 
under the Large Load Interconnection Process described in Appendix IV to the Participating 
TO’s Tariff. 
Participating TO’s Tariff shall mean the currently effective tariff of DCR Transmission, 
L.L.C., Transmission Owner Tariff on file with FERC, as may be amended or superseded from 
time to time upon further filing with and acceptance by FERC.  
Participating TO’s Transmission System shall mean the facilities owned and operated 
by the Participating TO and that have been placed under the CAISO’s Operational Control, 
which facilities form part of the CAISO Controlled Grid. 
Party or Parties shall mean the Participating TO, CAISO, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer or the applicable combination of the above. 
Point of Interconnection shall mean the point, as set forth in Appendix A to this LLIA, 
where the Interconnection Facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Transmission System. 
Reasonable Efforts shall mean, with respect to an action required to be attempted or 
taken by a Party under this LLIA, efforts that are timely and consistent with Good Utility 
Practice and are otherwise substantially equivalent to those a Party would use to protect its own 
interests. 
Scoping Meeting shall mean the meeting among representatives of the  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO(s), and, as applicable the CAISO and other 
Affected Systems, conducted for the purpose of discussing alternative interconnection options, to 
exchange information including any transmission data and earlier study evaluations that would 
be reasonably expected to impact such interconnection options, to analyze such information, and 
to determine the potential feasible Points of Interconnection. 
System Protection Facilities shall mean the equipment, including necessary protection 
signal communications equipment, that protects: (1) the Participating TO’s Transmission 
System, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, CAISO Controlled Grid, and Affected 
Systems from faults or other electrical disturbances occurring at the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities; and (2) the Large Load Facility from faults or other 
electrical system disturbances occurring on the CAISO Controlled Grid, Participating TO’s
--- PAGE 95 ---
8 
Interconnection Facilities, and Affected Systems or on other delivery systems or other generating 
systems to which the CAISO Controlled Grid is directly connected.  
Transmission Control Agreement shall mean CAISO FERC Electric Tariff No. 7. 
Trial Operation shall mean the period during which the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer is engaged in on-site test operations and commissioning of the Large Load Facility 
prior to Commercial Operation. 
Article 2. Effective Date, Term and Termination 
2.1 
Effective Date. This LLIA shall become effective upon execution by all Parties subject 
to acceptance by FERC (if applicable), or if filed unexecuted, upon the date specified by 
FERC. The Participating TO shall promptly file this LLIA with FERC upon execution in 
accordance with Article 3.1, if required. 
2.2 
Term of Agreement. Subject to the provisions of Article 2.3, this LLIA shall remain in 
effect for a period of ten (10) years from the Effective Date (Term Specified in individual 
agreements to be ten (10) years or such other longer period as the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may request) and shall be automatically renewed for each 
successive one-year period thereafter. 
2.3 
Termination Procedures. 
2.3.1 Written Notice. This LLIA may be terminated by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer after giving the Participating TO and, as applicable CAISO, ninety (90) 
Calendar Days advance written notice, or by the Participating TO notifying FERC 
after the Large Load Facility permanently ceases Commercial Operation. 
2.3.2 Default. A Party may terminate this LLIA in accordance with Article 17. 
2.3.3 Suspension of Work. This LLIA may be deemed terminated in accordance with 
Article 5.14, if applicable. 
2.3.4 Notwithstanding Articles 2.3.1, 2.3.2, and 2.3.3, no termination shall become 
effective until the Parties have complied with all Applicable Laws and Regulations 
applicable to such termination, including the filing with FERC of a notice of 
termination of this LLIA (if applicable), which notice has been accepted for filing 
by FERC, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer has fulfilled its 
termination cost obligations under Article 2.4. 
2.4 
Termination Costs. Immediately upon the other Parties’ receipt of a notice of the 
termination of this LLIA pursuant to Article 2.3 above, the Participating TO will 
determine the total cost responsibility of the Large Load Interconnection Customer. If, as 
of the date of the other Parties’ receipt of the notice of termination, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer has not already paid its share of Network Upgrade costs, as set 
forth in Appendix G to this LLIA, the Participating TO will liquidate the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Financial Security associated with its cost
--- PAGE 96 ---
9 
responsibility for Network Upgrades, in accordance with Appendix IV, Attachment 4 of 
the Participating TO’s tariff. 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer will also be responsible for all costs incurred 
or irrevocably committed to be incurred in association with the construction of the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities (including any cancellation costs relating to 
orders or contracts for Interconnection Facilities and equipment) and other such expenses, 
including any Distribution Upgrades for which the Participating TO has incurred 
expenses or has irrevocably committed to incur expenses and has not been reimbursed by 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer, as of the date of the other Parties’ receipt of 
the notice of termination, subject to the limitations set forth in this Article 2.4. Nothing in 
this Article 2.4 shall limit the Parties’ rights under Article 17. If, as of the date of the 
other Parties’ receipt of the notice of termination, the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer has not already reimbursed the Participating TO for costs incurred to construct 
the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, the Participating TO will liquidate the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Financial Security associated 
with the construction of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities. If the amount 
of the Interconnection Financial Security liquidated by the Participating TO under this 
Article 2.4 is insufficient to compensate the Participating TO for actual costs associated 
with the construction of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities contemplated in 
this Article, any additional amounts will be the responsibility of the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer. Any such additional amounts due from the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer beyond the amounts covered by its Interconnection Financial 
Security will be due to the Participating TO immediately upon termination of this LLIA. 
If the amount of the Interconnection Financial Security exceeds the Interconnection 
Customer’s cost responsibility under this LLIA, any excess amount will be released to the 
Interconnection Customer. 
2.4.1 Large Load Interconnection.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, in the event of 
termination by a Party, all Parties shall use commercially Reasonable Efforts to 
mitigate the costs, damages, and charges arising as a consequence of termination. 
With respect to any portion of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities that 
have not yet been constructed or installed, the Participating TO shall to the extent 
possible, and with the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s authorization, cancel 
any pending orders of, or return, any materials or equipment for, or contracts for 
construction of, such facilities; provided that in the event the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer elects not to authorize such cancellation, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall assume all payment obligations with respect to such 
materials, equipment, and contracts, and the Participating TO shall deliver such 
material and equipment, and, if necessary, assign such contracts, to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer as soon as practicable, at the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s expense. To the extent that the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
has already paid the Participating TO for any or all such costs of materials or 
equipment not taken by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Participating 
TO shall promptly refund such amounts to the Large Load Interconnection
--- PAGE 97 ---
10 
Customer, less any costs, including penalties, incurred by the Participating TO to 
cancel any pending orders of, or return such materials, equipment, or contracts. 
2.4.2 The Participating TO may, at its option, retain any portion of such materials, 
equipment, or facilities that the Large Load Interconnection Customer chooses not 
to accept delivery of, in which case the Participating TO shall be responsible for all 
costs associated with procuring such materials, equipment, or facilities. 
2.4.3 With respect to any portion of the Interconnection Facilities, and any other facilities 
already installed or constructed pursuant to the terms of this LLIA, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall be responsible for all costs associated with the 
removal, relocation or other disposition or retirement of such materials, equipment, 
or facilities. 
2.5 
Disconnection. Upon termination of this LLIA, the Parties will take all appropriate steps 
to disconnect the Large Load Facility from the Participating TO’s Transmission System. 
All costs required to effectuate such disconnection shall be borne by the terminating 
Party, unless such termination resulted from the non-terminating Party’s Default of this 
LLIA or such non-terminating Party otherwise is responsible for these costs under this 
LLIA. 
2.6 
Survival. This LLIA shall continue in effect after termination to the extent necessary to 
provide for final billings and payments and for costs incurred hereunder, including 
billings and payments pursuant to this LLIA; to permit the determination and 
enforcement of liability and indemnification obligations arising from acts or events that 
occurred while this LLIA was in effect; and to permit each Party to have access to the 
lands of the other Parties pursuant to this LLIA or other applicable agreements, to 
disconnect, remove or salvage its own facilities and equipment. 
Article 3. Regulatory Filings and CAISO Tariff Compliance 
3.1 
Filing. The Participating TO shall file this LLIA (and any amendment hereto) with the 
appropriate Governmental Authority(ies), if required. The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer may request that any information provided be subject to the confidentiality 
provisions of Article 22. If the Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed this 
LLIA, or any amendment thereto, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
reasonably cooperate with the Participating TO and, as applicable, CAISO with respect to 
such filing and to provide any information reasonably requested by the Participating TO 
or CAISO as needed to comply with applicable regulatory requirements. 
3.2 
Agreement Subject to CAISO Tariff. The Large Load Interconnection Customer will 
comply with all applicable provisions of the CAISO Tariff, including the GIDAP as may 
be required pursuant to Appendix H for co-located or Behind-the-Meter Generation. 
Article 4. Scope of Service 
4.1 
Large Load Interconnection Service. Large Load Interconnection Service allows the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer to connect the Large Load Facility to the
--- PAGE 98 ---
11 
Participating TO’s Transmission System and be eligible to receive energy from the 
CAISO Controlled Grid. To the extent the Large Load Interconnection Customer wants 
to receive Large Load Interconnection Service, the Participating TO shall construct 
facilities identified in Appendices A and C that the Participating TO is responsible to 
construct. 
4.2 
Provision of Service. The Participating TO shall provide Interconnection Service for the 
Large Load Facility. Any Interconnection Service subject to Appendix H shall be 
negotiated between the Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection Customer and the 
CAISO.  
4.3 
Performance Standards. Each Party shall perform all of its obligations under this LLIA 
in accordance with Applicable Laws and Regulations, Applicable Reliability Standards, 
and Good Utility Practice, and to the extent a Party is required or prevented or limited in 
taking any action by such regulations and standards, such Party shall not be deemed to be 
in Breach of this LLIA for its compliance therewith. If such Party is the Participating TO, 
then that Party shall amend the LLIA and submit the amendment to FERC for approval. 
4.4 
No Transmission Service. The execution of this LLIA does not constitute a request for, 
nor the provision of, any transmission service over the Participating TO’s Transmission 
System, and does not convey any right to deliver electricity to, or receive electricity from, 
any specific point of delivery. Large Load Interconnection Customer must request 
transmission service from CAISO, including through a Scheduling Coordinator, in 
accordance with the CAISO Tariff.  
Article 5. Large Load Interconnect Customer’s Interconnection Facilities Engineering, 
Procurement, And Construction 
5.1 
Design Requirements.  Interconnection Facilities, Network Upgrades, and Distribution 
Upgrades shall be studied, designed, and constructed pursuant to Good Utility Practice. 
Such studies, design and construction shall be based on the assumed accuracy and 
completeness of all technical information received by the Participating TO from the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer associated with interconnecting the Large Load 
Facility. 
5.1.1 Standard Option. The Participating TO shall design, procure, and construct the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Network Upgrades using Reasonable 
Efforts to complete the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Network 
Upgrades by the dates set forth in Appendix B, Milestones. The Participating TO 
shall not be required to undertake any action which is inconsistent with its standard 
safety practices, its material and equipment specifications, its design criteria and 
construction procedures, its labor agreements, and Applicable Laws and 
Regulations. In the event the Participating TO reasonably expects that it will not be 
able to complete the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Network 
Upgrades, and Distribution Upgrades by the specified dates, the Participating TO 
shall promptly provide written notice to the Large Load Interconnection Customer
--- PAGE 99 ---
12 
and, as applicable, the CAISO and the Participating TO shall undertake Reasonable 
Efforts to meet the earliest dates thereafter.    
5.2 
Liquidated Damages. The actual damages to the Large Load Interconnection Customer, 
in the event the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities or Network Upgrades are 
not completed by the dates designated by the Large Load Interconnection Customer and 
accepted by the Participating TO, may include Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
fixed operation and maintenance costs and lost opportunity costs. Such actual damages 
are uncertain and impossible to determine at this time. Because of such uncertainty, any 
liquidated damages paid by the Participating TO the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer in the event that the Participating TO does not complete any portion of the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities or Network Upgrades by the applicable 
dates, shall be an amount equal to one half of one percent (½ of 1%) per day of the actual 
cost of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Network Upgrades, in the 
aggregate, for which the Participating TO has assumed responsibility to design, procure 
and construct. 
However, in no event shall the total liquidated damages exceed twenty percent (20%) of 
the actual cost of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Network Upgrades 
for which the Participating TO has assumed responsibility to design, procure, and 
construct. The foregoing payments will be made by the Participating TO the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer as just compensation for the damages caused to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, which actual damages are uncertain and impossible to 
determine at this time, and as reasonable liquidated damages, but not as a penalty or a 
method to secure performance of this LLIA. Liquidated damages, when the Parties agree 
to them, are the exclusive remedy for the Participating TO’s failure to meet its schedule. 
No liquidated damages shall be paid to the Large Load Interconnection Customer if: (1) 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer is not ready to commence use of the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities or Network Upgrades to take the delivery of 
power for the Large Load Facility’s Trial Operation, unless the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer would have been able to commence use of the Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities or Network Upgrades to take the delivery of power for 
Large Load Facility’s Trial Operation, but for the Participating TO’s delay; and (2) the 
Participating TO’s failure to meet the specified dates is the result of the action or inaction 
of the Large Load Interconnection Customer or any other interconnection customer who 
has entered into an interconnection agreement with the Participating TO and/or CAISO, 
action or inaction by the CAISO, or any cause beyond the Participating TO’s reasonable 
control or reasonable ability to cure.   
In no event shall the Participating TO have any responsibility or liability to the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer for liquidated damages pursuant to the provisions of this 
Article 5.2.

Reserved.
